09. [&#8202;](#refnote-117)[↑](#ref-back-117-0)
    show

    Text of the document which appeared in the _Manila Gazette_ on April 23, 1898

    Further Notes:
    1\. This is the English language text of the document as published by the supporting source cited, possibly as translated from the original Spanish or Tagalog. In 1898, Spanish, Tagalog, and English were official languages in the Spanish colonial Philippines.[&#8202;](#ref-back-113-0)[\[105\]](#refnote-113)2\. In the Spanish colonial Philippiines, the term _Filipino_ was reserved for full-blooded Spaniards born in the Philippines ( _insulares_). Full-blooded Spaniards born in the Spanish peninsula were termed _peninsulares_. The Filipinos that we know today were then termed _indios_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-114-0)[\[106\]](#refnote-114)[&#8202;](#ref-back-115-0)[\[107\]](#refnote-115)

    The text of the document as published in the cited source was as follows:

    > OFFICE OF THE GOVERNMENT AND OF THE CAPTAIN-GENERAL OF THE PHILIPPINES
    >
    > Fellow Spaniards,
    >
    > Hostilities between Spain and the United States have broken out.
    >
    > The moment has come for us to show the world that we are more than courageous to triumph over those, who, feigning to be loyal friends, took advantage of our misfortunes and capitalized on our nobility by making use of the means civilized nations consider as condemnable and contemptible.
    >
    > The Americans, gratified with their social progress, have drained off our patience and have instigated the war through wicked tactics, treacherous acts, and violations of human rights and internal agreements.
    >
    > Fighting will be short and decisive. God of victories will render this victory glorious and complete as demanded by reason and justice to our cause.
    >
    > Spain, counting on the sympathies of all nations, will come out in triumph from this new test, by shattering and silencing the adventurers of those countries which, without cohesiveness and post, offer to humanity shameful traditions and the ungrateful spectacle of some embassies within which jointly dwell intrigues and defamation, cowardice and cynicism.
    >
    > A US squadron, manned by strangers, by ignorant undisciplined men, is coming into the Archipelago for the purpose of grabbing from us what we consider to be our life, honor freedom. It tries to inspire (motivate) American sailors by saying that we are weak, they are encouraged to keep on with an undertaking that can be accomplished; namely of substituting the Catholic religion with Protestantism, they consider you as a people who impedes growth; they will seize your wealth as if you do not know your rights to property; they will snatch away from you those they consider as useful to man their ships, to be exploited as workers in their fields and factories.
    >
    > Useless plans! Ridiculous boastings!
    >
    > Your indomitable courage suffices to hold off those who dare to bring it to reality. We know you will not allow them to mock the faith you are professing, their feet to step on the temple of the true God, incredulity to demolish the sacred images you honor; you will not allow the invaders to desecrate the tombs of your forefathers; to satisfy their immodest passions at the expense of your wives and daughters' honor; you will not allow them to seize all the properties you have put up through honest work in order to assure your future; you will not allow them to commit any of those crimes inspired by their wickedness and greed, because your bravery and patriotism suffice in scaring them away and knocking down the people who, calling themselves civilized and cultured, resort to the extermination of the natives of North America instead of trying to attract them to live a civilized life and of progress.
    >
    > Filipinos! Prepare yourself for the battle and united together under the glorious Spanish flag, always covered with laurels, let us fight, convinced that victory will crown our efforts and let us reply the intimations of our enemies with a decision befitting a Christian and patriot, with a cry of "Long live Spain!"
    >
    > Manila, April 23, 1898
    >
    > Your general
    > BASILO AUGISTIN[&#8202;](#ref-back-116-0)[\[108\]](#refnote-116)

10. [&#8202;](#refnote-120)[↑](#ref-back-120-0)The American squadron consisted of nine ships: _Olympia_ (flagship), [_Boston_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Boston_(1884) "USS Boston (1884)"), [_Baltimore_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Baltimore_(C-3) "USS Baltimore (C-3)"), [_Raleigh_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Raleigh_(C-8) "USS Raleigh (C-8)"), [_Concord_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Concord_(PG-3) "USS Concord (PG-3)"), [_Petrel_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Petrel_(PG-2) "USS Petrel (PG-2)"), [_McCulloch_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_McCulloch "USS McCulloch"), _Zapphire_, and _Nashan_. The Spanish squadron consisted of seven ships: [_Reina Cristina_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_cruiser_Reina_Cristina "Spanish cruiser Reina Cristina") (flagship), [_Castilla_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_cruiser_Castilla "Spanish cruiser Castilla"), [_Don Juan de Austria_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_cruiser_Don_Juan_de_Austria "Spanish cruiser Don Juan de Austria"), [_Don Antonio de Ulloa_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_cruiser_Don_Antonio_de_Ulloa "Spanish cruiser Don Antonio de Ulloa"), [_Isla de Luzon_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_cruiser_Isla_de_Luz%C3%B3n "Spanish cruiser Isla de Luzón"), [_Isla de Cuba_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Isla_de_Cuba#Spanish_Navy "USS Isla de Cuba"), and _Marques del Duero_. The Spanish ships were of inferior quality to the American ships; _Castilla_ was unpowered and had to be towed into position by the transport ship _Manila_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-119-0)[\[110\]](#refnote-119)
11. [&#8202;](#refnote-150)[↑](#ref-back-150-0)The [Springfield Model 1892–99](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield_Model_1892%E2%80%9399 "Springfield Model 1892–99") was a [Krag–Jørgensen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krag%E2%80%93J%C3%B8rgensen "Krag–Jørgensen") rifle manufactured under license in the U.S. by the [Springfield Armor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield_Armor "Springfield Armor"). It was commonly referred to as a "Krag".
12. [&#8202;](#refnote-168)[↑](#ref-back-168-0)Hobson resigned his commission after his request to retire as a [naval captain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captain_(United_States_O-6) "Captain (United States O-6)") was refused, prompting [Secretary of the Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Secretary_of_the_Navy "United States Secretary of the Navy") [William Henry Moody](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Henry_Moody "William Henry Moody") to reconsider and allow him to retire.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Hobson-0)[\[156\]](#refnote-Hobson) He was elected in 1907 from [Alabama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alabama "Alabama") to the [U.S. House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._House_of_Representatives "U.S. House of Representatives"). In 1934, his naval retirement rank was advanced to [rear admiral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rear_Admiral_(United_States) "Rear Admiral (United States)").
13. [&#8202;](#refnote-172)[↑](#ref-back-172-0)Painting is signed with initials A.A. by Antonio Antón and Antonio Iboleón. It is an idealized view — since the ships represented never sailed together — of the Spanish Squadron of Instruction steaming in a choppy sea under partly cloudy skies in 1896, before the Spanish-American War of 1898. On the left is the [battleship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battleship "Battleship") [_Pelayo_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_battleship_Pelayo "Spanish battleship Pelayo") with [ensign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naval_ensign "Naval ensign"), followed by the armored cruisers [_Cristóbal Colón_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_cruiser_Crist%C3%B3bal_Col%C3%B3n "Spanish cruiser Cristóbal Colón") and [_Infanta Maria Teresa_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_cruiser_Infanta_Maria_Teresa "Spanish cruiser Infanta Maria Teresa") and the [protected cruiser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protected_cruiser "Protected cruiser") [_Alfonso XIII_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_cruiser_Alfonso_XIII "Spanish cruiser Alfonso XIII"); on the right are the armored cruisers [_Emperador Carlos V_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_cruiser_Emperador_Carlos_V "Spanish cruiser Emperador Carlos V") (with ensign), [_Almirante Oquendo_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_cruiser_Almirante_Oquendo "Spanish cruiser Almirante Oquendo"), and [_Vizcaya_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_cruiser_Vizcaya "Spanish cruiser Vizcaya"). The [torpedo gunboat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torpedo_gunboat "Torpedo gunboat") [_Destructor_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_warship_Destructor "Spanish warship Destructor") steams on the [starboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_and_starboard "Port and starboard") side of _Pelayo_. Two [_Furor_-class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Furor-class_destroyer "Furor-class destroyer") [destroyers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destroyer "Destroyer") steam along the [bows](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bow_(watercraft) "Bow (watercraft)") of _Emperador Carlos V_.
